# Nani jo Ghar: the recording sheet

**The one central recording sheet** (Zafar, 14 Nov): every question for Mum and everything still to record, in one place. Updated 14 November 2026.

**Grammar first**, a table for each kind of question, **then each game**, a table each. In every table the rows still to do come first (needs Mum's Kutchi, then Kutchi known: just record, then to confirm); the rows that are done stay below them, in grey in the Word copy, so nothing is ever lost.

**At the sitting:** say the row's ID before each line (it lines the recording up with this sheet); say each line the way you would at home; if the family just uses the English word, say so. **English only:** the Kutchi column holds only what Mum (or the family) has said; nothing here is made up.

*Made from `data/lang/recording-sheet.json` (one row per question; only ever added to) by `node build/tools/lang/recording-sheet.mjs`; `node build/tools/ops/recordings.mjs` adds what the language engine needs. Never edit this file or the Word copy by hand: ask for the sheet and it is pulled from that source.*

| CK-ALL-95 | Which dishes are really ours?: These are the dishes we'd like in the game, a mix of Kutch cooking and the East African food we grew up with. For each: do you really make it, what's it called at home, and what would you add, remove or change? Which are more Kenya or Tanzania, and which are more Kutch? And what's missing that you'd expect in your kitchen? Chai · maani / rotli · daal · chaat bowl (chana bateta) · samosa · mishkaki with chips · chips mayai · mogo · makai (corn on the cob) · mandazi · dahi puri / sev puri · dabeli · falooda · jalebi · sheer khurma · biryani / pilau For the ones you really make, name five to ten things that go in each, and say if it belongs to an occasion (Eid morning, everyday dinner, guests, a rainy day). | Kitchen talk (about 20–30 minutes, relaxed) | Needs Mum's Kutchi |  | Round 4 I36; Round 3 I36; Combined I36 |
| CK-ALL-96 | How you make the first six: For each, talk through what goes in and in what order, what you do at each step, and how you know it's ready. This becomes the recipe in the game. 1. Chai: water, tea, boil, milk, sugar? Masala, elchi, ginger? When do they go in? 2. Maani / rotli: flour, water, oil or ghee? How thin? When do you flip it? Ghee on top? 3. Daal: which daal? What goes in the tadka and in what order (jeeru, rai, hing, garlic, chilli…)? Onion, tomato? 4. Chaat bowl: every topping, in the usual order. 5. Samosa: which filling? How do you fold it? 6. Mishkaki: which meat, what else goes on the skewer, the marinade, the chips on the side with chilli and lemon? | Kitchen talk (about 20–30 minutes, relaxed) | Needs Mum's Kutchi |  | Round 4 I37; Round 3 I37; Combined I37 |
| CK-ALL-97 | Who likes what: In the game everyone has their own taste and the child has to listen. Tell us the real ones, silly ones included: who takes three sugars, who has no milk, who won't touch chilli, who always wants extra onion, who hates what. | Kitchen talk (about 20–30 minutes, relaxed) | Needs Mum's Kutchi |  | Round 4 I38; Round 3 I38; Combined I38 |
| CK-ALL-98 | A real chai order, in one breath: Finally, say two or three complete orders the way they'd be said at home: "chai with two sugars, no milk, half a cup", "two maani and daal, no onion", "one veg skewer and one mixed, with chips". These are the sentences the game will copy. | Kitchen talk (about 20–30 minutes, relaxed) | Needs Mum's Kutchi |  | Round 4 I39; Round 3 I39; Combined I39 |
